# Technical Research: Sub-orchestrator / coordinator mode (--sub) for the proposed /sc:orchestrator

## Task Overview

GitHub issue #444 asks for an evaluation, before anyone builds it, of whether the proposed `/sc:orchestrator` command should support a sub-orchestrator or coordinator mode (possibly selected with `--sub`): a session that delegates work to its own sessions inside a worktree while taking orders from, and reporting to, an orchestrator above. Issues #339 (v1 of `/sc:orchestrator`) and #423 (v2) depend on the answer. PR #449 named the role "a coordinator under another orchestrator" in the installed rules without defining it, and deliberately deferred rule WC-13 to this issue (INF-027). The requester wants a decision report and explicitly does NOT want an implementation plan or any implementation started.

This task runs the `tech-research` Deep-tier pipeline: 8 parallel codebase investigations (Phase 2), a 5-lens-type research completeness gate with serialized fixes (Phase 3), 4 gap-driven web investigations (Phase 4), 6 synthesis files and a synthesis gate (Phase 5), then single-agent assembly of `RESEARCH-REPORT-sub-orchestrator-mode.md` followed by a lens-based report gate, a source-document fidelity gate and a mandatory anti-omission gate (Phase 6), and presentation and completion (Phase 7). The whole task is read-only: no source, doc, test, hook or config file is edited, no GitHub write is made, and no Orca state is changed. The report keeps all 10 sections, but Section 8 is decision-only (N/A as an implementation plan; it carries a "Change surface per option" table instead).

## Key Objectives

The following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:

1. **Evidence base:** Eight codebase research files (`research/01-*.md` to `research/08-*.md`) and four web research files (`research/web-01-*.md` to `research/web-04-*.md`) that re-verify every cited file:line against the current code and tag every documentation-sourced claim [CODE-VERIFIED], [CODE-CONTRADICTED] or [UNVERIFIED].
2. **Decision report:** `RESEARCH-REPORT-sub-orchestrator-mode.md` with all 10 sections, an Options Analysis covering O1-O6, and a Section 7 recommendation that is a decision with explicit, testable gates (what must be true before a `--sub` mode is built), covering value, implications, unlocks and rethinks (WC-13, decision D5, and the PR #449 role "a coordinator under another orchestrator").
3. **Change surface, not a plan:** Section 8 marked N/A as an implementation plan ("the requester asked for a decision report, not a plan") and carrying a "Change surface per option" table in which every row names real files, tests, docs and installed copies.
4. **Quality gates passed:** Research gate, synthesis gate, lens-based report gate, source-document fidelity gate and anti-omission gate all reach PASS under the serialized fix protocol (max 2 fix cycles each), with open blocking findings escalated to the user rather than passed.
5. **Gaps recorded honestly:** `gaps-and-questions.md` and Section 9 list everything UNVERIFIED, including facts that need a live Orca run (a live run is out of scope for this read-only investigation).

CRITICAL — Documentation Staleness Protocol: documentation describes intent or historical state, code describes CURRENT state, so cross-validate EVERY structural claim from documentation against actual code before reporting it as current: (a) components or services described in docs, verify the directory, entry file and key classes exist (use Glob); (b) pipelines or call chains, trace at least the first and last hop in source; (c) file paths mentioned in docs, spot-check they exist and note discrepancies; (d) commands, flags, hooks or endpoints, verify them in the actual code. Re-read every file:line you cite yourself, because line numbers in scope files and earlier analyses are second-hand, and prior findings F1-F7 and G1-G4 are hypotheses to confirm or refute, not evidence. Output Format: descriptive headers per file or logical group, actual file paths, class and function names and line numbers, anomalies and tech debt, '**[STALE DOC]**' markers on stale documentation, a 'Key Takeaways' bullet list at the end of each section, and at the end of the file '## Gaps and Questions' (things needing further investigation plus all UNVERIFIED and CODE-CONTRADICTED claims), '## Stale Documentation Found', and '## Summary' (3-5 sentences). Content rules: tables over prose, file:line citations, never reproduce full source code, document only what you verified, do not guess or infer. [FULL COMMON BLOCK BELOW] CRITICAL — Incremental File Writing Protocol (violation results in data loss): (1) FIRST ACTION: create your output file immediately with Write containing this header: '# Research: [Your Topic]', '**Investigation type:** [type]', '**Scope:** [files/directories assigned]', '**Status:** In Progress', '**Date:** [today]', then '---'; (2) as you investigate each file, component or logical unit, IMMEDIATELY append your findings to the output file using Edit and never accumulate findings in your context window; (3) the file growing after each append is correct, never rewrite it from scratch; (4) when finished, update the Status line from 'In Progress' to 'Complete' and append a summary section. Research Protocol: (1) read actual source files, what each does, exports and imports; (2) trace data flow; (3) document the implementation with file paths and line numbers; (4) identify patterns and conventions; (5) check edge cases, error handling, fallbacks; (6) note dependencies in both directions; (7) flag gaps; (8) note integration opportunities. CRITICAL — Documentation Staleness Protocol: documentation describes intent or historical state, code describes CURRENT state, so cross-validate EVERY structural claim from documentation against actual code before reporting it as current: (a) components or services described in docs, verify the directory, entry file and key classes exist (use Glob); (b) pipelines or call chains, trace at least the first and last hop in source; (c) file paths mentioned in docs, spot-check they exist and note discrepancies; (d) commands, flags, hooks or endpoints, verify them in the actual code. Mark EVERY doc-sourced claim as [CODE-VERIFIED] (confirmed by reading source at file:line), [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (code shows something different: describe what the code shows) or [UNVERIFIED] (no corresponding code found, or needs a live Orca run); issue and PR text is documentation too, so tag every claim taken from an issue or comment against the code it describes; [UNVERIFIED] and [CODE-CONTRADICTED] claims MUST appear in the Gaps and Questions section; never present a doc-sourced claim as verified fact without a tag; documentation is NOT verification, only reading the actual source verifies. Re-read every file:line you cite yourself, because line numbers in scope files and earlier analyses are second-hand, and prior findings F1-F7 and G1-G4 are hypotheses to confirm or refute, not evidence. Output Format: descriptive headers per file or logical group, actual file paths, class and function names and line numbers, anomalies and tech debt, '**[STALE DOC]**' markers on stale documentation, a 'Key Takeaways' bullet list at the end of each section, and at the end of the file '## Gaps and Questions' (things needing further investigation plus all UNVERIFIED and CODE-CONTRADICTED claims), '## Stale Documentation Found', and '## Summary' (3-5 sentences). Content rules: tables over prose, file:line citations, never reproduce full source code, document only what you verified, do not guess or infer. TASK-SPECIFIC CONSTRAINTS (non-negotiable): (1) READ-ONLY investigation: you MUST NOT edit any source, doc, test, hook or config file; the only file you may create or edit is your own output file named above; no GitHub writes (only read-only gh calls, always with --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude, never the upstream SuperClaude-Org repository); no Orca state changes (only --help, skills get, list/show/read-style commands and file reads; NEVER run worker-start, dispatch, task-create, worktree create, agent launch, run-create, or change any setting); (2) do NOT pass a model parameter on any Agent call you make and do not run /model; (3) you are a subagent: do not use SendMessage, TaskCreate, TaskUpdate or TeamCreate, and end with a one-line completion statement." - [x] Spawn an Agent (subagent_type "rf-task-researcher"; do NOT pass a `model` parameter) with the quoted prompt below to check the feasibility of the capability-narrowing grant sketched in issue #339 Step 3 against the primary sources, ensuring the output file `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RESEARCH-sub-orchestrator-mode-20261108-181115/research/web-03-capability-narrowing-tokens.md` exists with `Status: Complete`, every finding carries a source URL, a HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW relevance rating, a relationship to the codebase and a reliability rating, a feasibility table (mechanism, offline verification, attenuation by a middle holder, fit inside hook-based enforcement, and what our quoted-grant precedent already covers), and ends with `## Key External Findings` and `## Recommendations from External Research`, with no claim lacking a URL and no file other than the output file created or modified. Output format: the final report MUST contain these 10 sections in this order: 1 Problem Statement; 2 Current State Analysis (every claim cites file paths and line numbers); 3 Target State; 4 Gap Analysis (table Gap / Current State / Target State / Severity / Notes); 5 External Research Findings (findings with source URLs and relevance ratings); 6 Options Analysis (options O1-O6, each with an assessment table, plus a comparison table); 7 Recommendation (a decision with explicit testable gates: value, implications, unlocks, rethinks, decision gates); 8 Implementation Plan, which for THIS report is decision-only: keep the heading '## 8. Implementation Plan', keep the statement 'N/A as an implementation plan — the requester asked for a decision report, not a plan.' and the 'Change surface per option' tables from synth-05, and add NO phases, NO step ordering, NO effort schedule and NO integration checklist; 9 Open Questions (table # / Question / Impact / Suggested Resolution); 10 Evidence Trail (tables listing all research, web research and synthesis files and the gaps log). Assembly rules: (1) write the report header first; (2) assemble sections in order, reading each synth file and writing its content into the correct position, writing each section to disk immediately; (3) generate the Table of Contents from the actual section headers after all sections are placed; (4) cross-check internal consistency: gaps in Section 4 are addressed by an option in Section 6, a gate or decision in Section 7, or an Open Question in Section 9 (Section 8 is N/A as a plan, so it does not carry step-level gap fixes); options in Section 6 reference evidence from Section 2 or 5; Open Questions in Section 9 are not answered elsewhere in the report; the Evidence Trail in Section 10 lists every research, web research, fix-addendum, scope and synthesis file (verify with Glob); the options and gates named in Section 7 exist in Section 6 and the options named in the Section 8 tables are the same six; (5) flag any contradiction between sections with '[CONTRADICTION: Component A claims X, Component B claims Y]' inline; (6) ensure no template placeholder text remains (search for TODO, TBD, PLACEHOLDER and unfilled template brackets; the evidence tags [CODE-VERIFIED], [CODE-CONTRADICTED], [UNVERIFIED], [STALE DOC] and markdown links are legitimate and must stay); (7) when the report is complete, write the assembler consistency note as a SEPARATE file `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RESEARCH-sub-orchestrator-mode-20261108-181115/qa/assembler-consistency-note.md` (create it with Write containing a header, then append with Edit; it lists every cross-check you performed under rule (4) and rule (6) with its result, and every contradiction you flagged inline, with the section where it sits; write 'none' if you flagged none); do NOT put the note inside the report and do NOT add a section or any other structure to the report for it. Content rules (non-negotiable): tables over prose for multi-item data; no full source-code reproductions, summarize with key signatures and file paths; ASCII diagrams for architecture and data flow, not prose; evidence cited inline as file:line; conciseness over comprehensiveness; every claim needs evidence, with no file path or URL it belongs in Open Questions; uncertainty marked explicitly with 'Unverified' or 'Open Question' markers. CRITICAL: you are assembling existing content, not creating new findings; preserve fidelity to the synthesis files and add only minimal transitional text; keep every evidence tag that the synth files carry; do NOT attempt full content validation, that is the QA agents' job; focus on assembly integrity: correct ordering, internal consistency, no placeholders, all components included. ### Open Questions

None for the builder. Intent was clear from the request: a read-only decision report with options, risks and a recommendation and no implementation plan. Interpretation applied and disclosed: Section 8 of the report becomes a decision-only "Change surface per option" section (see Builder Notes).

### Builder Notes (recorded by the task builder at build time)

- **Template and structure:** Template 02 (complex task). The template's `phase-outputs/` handoff tree is replaced by the native tech-research folder layout (`research/`, `synthesis/`, `qa/`, `reviews/`), as the BUILD_REQUEST requires; the Handoff File Convention section says so.
- **Phase 3 partitioning:** 8 codebase research files (scope files excluded) is more than 6, so each of the 5 gate agent types is split in 2 (files 01-04 and 05-08), giving 10 agents. The two cross-validation analyst instances also read the Summary, Key Takeaways and Gaps sections of the other partition so that cross-partition contradictions are still detected. Merging happens in the consolidation item.
- **Phase 5 partitioning:** 6 synthesis files is more than 4, so each of the 5 gate agent types is split in 2 (synth-01 to 03 and synth-04 to 06), giving 10 agents.
- **Phase 6 lens scaling (I19):** the report size is unknown at build time, so 4 structural + 4 content lenses are unconditional and a 5th structural lens (doc-claim-tagging) and a 5th content lens (evidence-proportionality) run only if the measured report exceeds 1500 lines (decided in `qa/phase6-report-stats.md`). Together with the 3 domain lenses this is 11 to 13 agents, inside the 9-13 range of Gate 3. The 5th lenses are task-specific additions allowed by I19.
- **Fidelity gate partitioning:** source files exceed 1000 lines, so 4 fidelity agents, each assigned two Phase 2 research files and the source files those cite. The M4 cross-source-contradiction agent is not spawned: source-to-source contradictions (the stale documentation) are already surfaced by the Phase 2 Documentation Staleness Protocol tags, by the cross-validation analysts in Phase 3 and by the doc-claim-tagging checks, and the BUILD_REQUEST specifies 2-4 fidelity agents.
- **Anti-omission gate partitioning:** research and synthesis inputs exceed ~1000 lines, so 4 agents: research 01-04; research 05-08; web files + research notes + scope files; synthesis files + gaps log + fix-addendum files. Every omission must be recovered or justified with a named report item or a quoted scope statement.
- **Fix cycles:** every gate encodes cycle 1 and a conditional cycle 2 as explicit checklist items (consolidate, one fix agent, structural and qualitative verification, decision), with the halt guards in the order regression, monotonicity, hard cap. Conditional items are skipped by logging, never left unchecked.
- **Post-completion QA (I17 items 5-6):** the Phase 6 lens gate, fidelity gate and anti-omission gate run on the final report and each is followed by its own verification round, so they serve as the post-completion lens-based QA and fidelity validation; Phase 7 adds the integrity checks. No separate duplicate gate was added.
- **No POST reflect gate:** the BUILD_REQUEST does not enable it, so no `POST_REFLECT_GATE` item exists; `reflect_pre` and `reflect_post` frontmatter fields are left empty. TESTING_REQUIREMENTS is N/A (documentation-only).
- **Staleness handling:** no item relies on a [CODE-CONTRADICTED] or [UNVERIFIED] claim from the BUILD_REQUEST; the known stale statements are named in the agent prompts only as statements to re-verify and not to rely on (the developer guide `--unattended` / `UNATTENDED_COMMANDS` statement is assigned to Agent 07 deliverable (d); the D5 RECOMMENDATION, CR-01 and option-a statements to Agent 06; the #422 and #446 statements to Agent 08).
- **Agent types:** Phase 2 and Phase 4 research agents use subagent_type "rf-task-researcher" because it has Write, Edit, Bash and the Tavily tools; synthesis and analyst agents use "rf-analyst"; QA agents use "rf-qa" and "rf-qa-qualitative"; assembly uses the dedicated "rf-assembler". No item passes a `model` parameter.
- **Date note:** the task id and `created_date` use 2026-11-08 (the id timestamp); the session context showed both 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09, so the executor must use the real current date when it writes `start_date` and `completion_date`.
- **Task-file size:** the file has about 120 checklist items because every agent, consolidation, fix and verification step is its own item (B2/I15/I20). Executors should process them in batches and re-read the file each time (F1 loop).
